# Cross-spec consequences queue

Each Wave 1 agent could edit only the files it owned, so it reported the changes needed elsewhere.
They are listed here grouped by the fold that raised them, not by target file.

**Status:** everything above the "Open items" section is applied: foundation targets in `7df8575`,
format targets and the management-surfaces analysis in the commit after `da0aecd`. Items asking for
a new spec are carried by `foundation.tsv`. Only the "Open items" section remains.

PyPI: yank/unyank file or release with optional reason, file delete, release delete. npm: unpublish version, unpublish package, deprecate/undeprecate. Galaxy: version delete, collection delete as one write.

Both: verdict supply-chain consumes.

Confirm a Package and its package-level document persist in head after every version is removed (retirement set depends on it). NOTE: async-operations.md (foundation queue) must be consistent with this entity - possibly this subsumes it.

## Start here: cross-cutting themes (read before the numbered items)

The numbered open items below were raised format by format. Many are the same finding arriving
from several formats; design each theme ONCE in its owning spec, then have every format cite it.

1. **Forward-moving freshness scoped to the pointer or the cache** (items 20, 22, 26, 27, 28, 30,
   32; seven formats). Clients ignore an older index, keep a newer cached revision, or drop any
   response whose `Last-Modified` is not later than their own clock. Exact-match `304` is not
   enough for curl-based clients (item 30). One data-model mechanism; debian's pointer-held signed
   wrapper and hackage's per-pointer TUF versions are instances. Qualifies data-model AC22.
2. **When a refusal actually binds** (items 16, 19, 21, 27, 28, 29, 31, 32, and the terraform,
   conan and puppet notes). Per format: does the client fall back to another repository, a
   mirror, or URLs inside our own metadata? The general answer from opam and openvsx: the registry
   regenerates every served document and owns every URL in it. supply-chain-policy states the
   per-format conditions.
3. **Reason-phrase-only clients need HTTP/1.1 on refusal routes** (item 28). A deployment
   constraint for the owed deployment spec.
4. **Clients that verify nothing** (conan, chef, alpine package signatures, luarocks, cpm and
   Carton, the editors). The registry's own verification is their only integrity check, which
   raises artifact-verification's priority and argues for serve-time verification (item 28).
5. **Advisory coverage gaps** (conda, terraform, conan, vagrant, chef, luarocks, cpan, puppet, arch
   have no usable OSV data; CPANSA, the Arch tracker and Homebrew's database exist outside OSV).
   Evidence for revisiting supply-chain-policy's single-feed decision.
6. **Patterned `pull` cannot run formats with a repository-wide index** (Open item 3, plus cargo,
   helm, dnf, zypper, conan). An addressed-object kind in auth.
7. **Completion-only fetch mode** (go-modules, nuget, maven, composer, cran, luarocks, openvsx,
   conan). One proxy-cache design with a verifier hook and an off-origin host allowlist.
8. **New credential presentation forms** (path-segment tokens: conda, terraform's capability,
   luarocks, openvsx; headers: X-NuGet-ApiKey, X-Jfrog-Art-Api, X-ApiKey, X-OpenVSX-Token;
   RSA-signed requests: chef). Consolidate in auth AC31, with redaction and plaintext refusal,
   inside AC10's external review scope.
9. **Virtual repositories are impossible for Hex** (item 12). A per-format capability.
10. **Dated**: HCP Vagrant shuts down 2026-12-31 (item 23).
11. **Outside this repository**: four probable bugs in the owner's Stackweaver registry found by
    terraform.md (module download 302 fails on all four clients; armored signatures accepted;
    per-platform upload overwrites SHA256SUMS; versions sorted as strings). Owner decides whether
    to file them there.

## Open items (raised by the format-side reconciliation, da0aecd)

**Items 1-3 applied to auth.md on 2026-09-27** (Q23 adopted: a fourth addressed-object kind, `descriptor`). Their follow-ups are items 33-38 below.

1. auth.md Pattern scopes, Cargo bullet: says cargo reports the registered spelling on every route; cargo.md now uses the folded crate key, with `{crate}/{version}` on version routes. Align auth.md to cargo.md.
2. auth.md: records a "Cargo yank uses push" divergence for the management-api author; cargo Q6 (adopted) resolved it from the cargo side: yank and unyank are bindings onto the management API's yank operation and need `delete`, like PyPI. Update auth.md's note. OWNER NOTE: both adopted under the delegation.
3. auth.md (judgment call, adopt under delegation): a "repository-wide but reveals no names" addressed-object kind. Without one, cargo's config.json and Helm's index.yaml report `none`, so a token with only a patterned pull cannot run cargo or helm at all.
4. management-api.md (when authored) must also carry: Helm's dispatch into the handler's write path so the index regenerates in the same snapshot, attaching a provenance file to an existing version with a coherence check, retirement, and per-format object reporting; pub's retraction (`delete`) and discontinuation (`push`); go-modules' deletion (410 plus retirement set); cargo's yank binding (`delete`).
5. Every format now renders policy refusals, but each exact response shape must be checked against captured traffic when that format's policy conformance case is written.

From maven.md (1ac78d6). auth.md client table: Gradle, sbt and Leiningen rows (Basic; challenge on GET, preemptive on PUT), same note on the mvn row. project-charter.md AC12: name Maven beside Helm as a consumer of the step-7 signing/index service. proxy-cache.md: Maven Central in the preconfigured set via the was-Q14 extension mechanism; Maven removal-table rows (immutability violation purges, a vanished artifact keeps and flags, SNAPSHOT pruning is ordinary); completion-only fetch mode (also requested by go-modules and nuget). management-api.md: delete version, delete file, prune SNAPSHOT builds, all `delete`, client-less. signing-service.md: unsigned generation of maven-metadata.xml at all three levels inside the triggering write, contention-safe (a captured two-deploy race lost a version when clients wrote it), virtual-repository merge, ETag from snapshot. artifact-verification.md: detached PGP .asc verification against a per-repository trust set. supply-chain-policy.md: Maven and Gradle show only the HTTP status line on 403, so a refusal's reason phrase is invisible there. management-surfaces analysis: a Maven row (no client triggers any management operation). catalogue.md: Ivy client reach unverified in this pass (maven AC19 is prove-or-strike).
12. From hex.md (9669f4b). FIRST FORMAT FOR WHICH VIRTUAL REPOSITORIES ARE IMPOSSIBLE: the repository name is inside the signed payload and clients check it against their configured name, so data-model.md's repository types and any "every format supports virtual" assumption (catalogue, charter, conformance matrix) need a per-format capability, likely via Capabilities(). A proxied hex.pm must serve upstream bytes unmodified under the client's `hexpm` name (re-signing is refused by every unmodified client, captured). auth.md client table: `mix` and `rebar3` rows (bare token, the scheme-less AC31 form). management-api.md: retire, unretire, revert, docs-delete on {name}/{version}; retirement is a deprecation needing `push`, revert needs `delete`. signing-service.md: one RSA key per hosted repository, RSA-SHA512 (public_key:sign/3 form) over the uncompressed payload, three documents signed synchronously inside the publish write, public key served as SPKI PEM at `public_key` with the OpenSSH SHA256 fingerprint, rotation as one re-sign-all write (client-visible: Hex pins one key), plus a verification entry for the proxied path against the upstream's configured key. upstream-adapters.md: per-upstream public key PEM (default hex.pm's), following the `installs` 301 to builds.hex.pm. conformance-harness.md: `state`-seeded hosted Hex versions must be signed through the signing service. proxy-cache.md removal table Hex rows: retirement and advisories ordinary, vanish keep-and-flag, checksum change purge and alert, signature failure never committed. supply-chain-policy.md: reason-phrase-invisible finding confirmed for two more clients; Hex is a second consumer of the Deps advisory read. Management-surfaces analysis: Hex row (retire and revert are client-driven).

Management-surfaces analysis: Conda row (all operations client-less except publish via rattler-build). NOTE for hex item 12: conda confirms virtual repositories ARE possible where nothing is signed with the repository name, so the capability is per format.

19. From rpm.md (a3c1e0f). Adds dnf and zypper to Open item 3: a pattern-restricted pull token cannot run them at all (repository-wide metadata lists every package name), same as cargo and helm. auth.md client rows: dnf (Basic sent up front; dnf5 sends NO credentials to key URLs and ignores sslcacert for them) and zypper (Basic only after a challenge, so WWW-Authenticate: Basic is mandatory). SECURITY: xml:base sends dnf to another host and dnf 4 sends the repository's credentials there over plain HTTP; hosted trees never emit it, proxied trees carrying it refused 502. signing-service.md: createrepo_c-equivalent gzip generation of a tree's metadata; RSA >= 3072 bits; armored SINGLE-packet signature (dnf5 refuses two packets); a key document listing old and new keys during rotation; retention of every checksum-named file referenced by any retained snapshot's repomd.xml (a republish that removed an old file broke a client mid-refresh, captured); the virtual merge. artifact-verification.md: repomd signature verification, RPM header and payload digest and signature verdicts (Alma 8 refuses Ed25519 package signatures; EL9 and EL10 refuse SHA-1), metalink checking. upstream-adapters.md: mirror-root joining, cross-host redirect allowlist, conditional revalidation, metalink fetch. management-api.md: batch publish as one write, advisory (updateinfo), comps and module operations, deletion. project-charter.md AC12: add Debian and RPM beside Helm and Maven as signing-service consumers. conformance-harness.md: seed path signs hosted RPM state; RPM cases remove the image's own repo files and install the CA system-wide. proxy-cache.md: RPM removal-table rows; a changed checksum at a cached location must serve the bytes each metadata revision names; range support required (dnf5 zchunk multi-range). supply-chain-policy.md: a per-repository OSV ecosystem declaration for OS-package formats (OSV keys Red Hat, Rocky, Alma, SUSE, openSUSE by release; Fedora is not an OSV ecosystem); fallback: refused packages do not fall back to other repositories, but a refused repomd.xml makes dnf5 (skip_if_unavailable default) and zypper skip our repository and install from others, so refusals are package-level only and the recommended client setup is a single baseurl. catalogue.md: prove the SUSE entry with the SLES BCI image, list the eight clients. 20. From debian.md (e77404f). CROSS-CUTTING DESIGN CHANGE: apt silently ignores a Release file OLDER than the one it already has (all four apt versions; apt source VerifyVendor), and revalidates only with If-Modified-Since, so a backwards Last-Modified gets a 304 and hides the change. A rollback served from a snapshot's stored signed Release is therefore invisible to every client that already updated. Adopted: index documents stay in the snapshot, but the signed wrapper (InRelease, Release, Release.gpg) belongs to the POINTER and is re-dated on every pointer move, monotonic per pointer. data-model.md must add a pointer-held signed wrapper stored outside snapshot content and QUALIFY AC22 (a promoted environment's signed wrapper is no longer byte-identical; content still is). Check whether other formats with signed, dated indexes (RPM repomd.xml, Alpine APKINDEX, Arch db, Hex) need the same rule. storage-and-gc.md (draft, pending re-gate): the fourth mark root must cover pointer-held wrappers and a virtual repository's merged documents. signing-service.md: key floor (trixie refuses SHA1 and RSA 1024; retires RSA 2048 in 2030), incremental generation, pointer-scoped wrapper, rotation with overlap (dual-signed Release accepted by clients holding either key), public key publication, verification entry, virtual merge; Release lists SHA256 only so the by-hash path equals the CAS key. management-api.md: publish, copy into suite (push), remove from suite and delete (delete), suite configuration and key rotation (admin only), dput binding (HTTP upload commits when the .changes arrives). auth.md: apt row (Basic on first request; over plain HTTP only an auth.conf entry that includes http://) and dput row. async-operations.md: virtual regeneration as deferred work. upstream-adapters.md: per-upstream OpenPGP keyring. proxy-cache.md: Debian removal-table rows. supply-chain-policy.md: OSV keys Debian and Ubuntu by SOURCE package and release (Debian:12), so the coordinate is source package, source version and a per-repository release qualifier; refusals leak when two sources offer the identical version (apt installs from the other with exit 0). conformance-harness.md: seed path signs hosted state; cases need a second source in the client and a hold on the client between two requests. catalogue.md: build the Pop!_OS client image from pinned ubuntu:24.04 (no official image); Mint and Pop apt is Ubuntu's. write-triggered-services-prototype.md: add a repoint case and the hash-set finding to Phase 3. Management-surfaces analysis: Debian row.
21. From alpine.md (99075e9). SECURITY: apk NEVER checks a package's own signature on a repository install (unsigned and foreign-key packages install from a trusted index); the protection is the index entry (C: SHA-1 of the control stream, S: size, datahash inside the control stream over the data). apk installs the HIGHEST version across all configured repositories, so dependency confusion is the default: supply-chain-policy.md and the operator docs must say so. URL userinfo credentials leak (apk-tools 2.14.12 `apk policy` prints them; a 301 warning prints them on both lines): hosted routes never redirect; docs recommend .netrc. Fallback matches rpm (item 19): a refused package does not fall back; a refused or untrusted index makes apk skip the repository silently. signing-service.md: APKINDEX.tar.gz generation with the signature as a prepended tar segment over exactly the index's own gzip stream, RSA 4096 per repository signing RSA256, key naming by exact filename in /etc/apk/keys, dual-signature rotation window (first trusted key decides), `A:` rewritten to the index's architecture as `apk index --rewrite-arch` does (an `A:noarch` entry sends clients to a 404), a signed index per declared architecture including empty ones. artifact-verification.md: apk v2 signed-stream verification (first-trusted-key rule; RSA, RSA256, RSA512) plus C:, S: and datahash checks. upstream-adapters.md: path joining under a mirror root, redirect allowlist forwarding credentials to the root host only, conditional revalidation, refusing http:// roots. management-api.md: batch publish per tree, delete version, delete package, set the architecture set, rotate the key, with grants. auth.md: apk row (userinfo sent preemptively; .netrc and HTTP_AUTH only after a 401; HTTP_AUTH not host-limited). conformance-harness.md: seed path calls the signing service; Alpine cases empty the image's /etc/apk/keys. supply-chain-policy.md: per-repository OSV declaration (Alpine:v3.N), matching by origin (source) package; no MAL records. Debian item 20 applies: check whether APKINDEX needs a pointer-scoped signed wrapper (its signature carries no date, so likely not; confirm in reconciliation).
22. From conan.md (4ac28af). SECOND FORMAT WITH THE ROLLBACK PROBLEM (after Debian item 20): the Conan 2 client keeps a newer cached revision over an older remote one (captured on 2.0.17 and 2.32.0), so a rollback is invisible. Adopted: serve the latest revision's time as no earlier than its pointer's last promotion or rollback. data-model.md: add a last-repoint time on Pointer and qualify AC22. RECONCILE ONCE: Debian needs a pointer-scoped re-dated signed wrapper, Conan a pointer-scoped revision time; define one general data-model mechanism (a monotonic per-pointer "moved at" that formats render into their own freshness signal) and have both specs cite it. SECURITY: the Conan client verifies NOTHING it downloads (a tampered package archive installed silently, captured), and the reference server serves a half-uploaded revision as latest; adopted: verify every file against its manifest before visibility or caching, and expose a revision only once its verified file set is complete. proxy-cache.md: a verifier hook on the completion-only fetch mode; Conan removal-table rows. upstream-adapters.md: ConanCenter adapter covering center2.conan.io and the frozen center.conan.io. management-api.md: six operations (two client-driven DELETE routes from `conan remove`, three reference-server-only routes, dropping abandoned incomplete revisions), all under `delete`; retirement set across a backwards repoint. auth.md: a `conan` row (token exchange at /v1/users/authenticate returns the registry token itself). Management-surfaces analysis: Conan row.
23. From vagrant.md (34a974d). DATED COMMITMENT: HCP Vagrant (vagrantcloud.com) stops creating boxes 2026-10-01 and stops operating 2026-12-31. The vagrantcloud.com real-traffic corpus must be recorded before 2026-12-31, and the spec's mirroring recipe (immediate sync, then freeze into a local repository) is the only way to keep HCP content. Add to HANDOFF.md "Open commitments that are easy to lose". auth.md: `vagrant` row (Bearer attached whenever the host matches VAGRANT_SERVER_URL, even over plain HTTP; --location-trusted forwards it to redirect targets); treat the `access_token` query parameter as credential material to redact; Vagrant sends one token to every repository on a host, so multi-repository users need the opt-in token (auth AC29). signing-service.md: an UNSIGNED consumer class: one generated catalog per box, default architecture entry last (2.3.7 ignores architecture and takes the last entry per provider: it installed arm64 on amd64, captured), relative URLs, no key. management-api.md: streamed, resumable upload sessions for multi-gigabyte boxes; one version with several provider files per publish as one write; the captured HCP publish sequence as input to a possible compatibility surface (not served in v1). upstream-adapters.md: a catalog path template; redirect chains limited to an allowlist (HCP goes 302, 302, 307 to presigned S3); upstream credential to the root host only. conformance-harness.md: seed path calls the index service; client images built from a checksum-verified .deb on a pinned base, pinned by the built image's digest; test CA into /opt/vagrant/embedded/cacert.pem; redaction covers userinfo, access_token and presigned query strings. format-handler-interface.md re-open: handler needs the externally visible base URL (npm needs it too). SECURITY: resume is keyed on the SHA-1 of the box URL, so if bytes change between an interrupted download and the resume they are spliced; without a checksum the corrupt image installs silently, and HCP publishes no checksums for bento. Adopted: a coordinate binds one set of bytes forever, and proxied boxes get our SHA-256 once cached. supply-chain-policy.md: no OSV Vagrant ecosystem (fourth format); refusals must answer HEAD and GET alike because direct URL lists fall back on GET failures.

Writes are signed with the client's RSA key (mixlib-authentication over SHA-1, protocols 1.0 and 1.1) with no field a token could travel in; private reads reach every URL only through JFrog's X-Jfrog-Art-Api header. Adopted: shares authenticated by registered RSA public keys verified centrally with the standard library (1.0, 1.1, 1.3), and X-Jfrog-Art-Api as a fifth token presentation form. auth.md: Chef client rows; the two new forms; AC31 extended; AC10's external review scope grows to cover canonical-string construction for signature checks; the shared layer checks the content hash at the end of the tarball part. credential-management.md (to author): a registered-public-key credential kind, key name sent as X-Ops-Userid. format-handler-interface.md re-open: an input for the end-of-part content-hash check; object taken from the multipart filename (Galaxy precedent). From puppet.md (409d44e). auth.md: `puppet` row (Bearer via forge_authorization; r10k's scheme-less token; Basic from userinfo). format-handler-interface.md: carve-out for a root-anchored /v3/ on hostnames bound to one repository (PDK always posts /v3/releases to the host root and drops any path; follows terraform's host-binding precedent). conformance-harness.md: the `repositories` setup key must express a hostname binding (terraform needs it too). management-api.md: seven operations (publish, deprecate, undeprecate, withdraw, restore, module delete, hard delete); withdrawal behaves like a yank (still installable when pinned) and takes `delete`; the Forge's PATCH and DELETE routes are bindings. artifact-verification.md: release-integrity entry for SHA-256 with an MD5 "weak" verdict (all three clients verify, but only against the server's own metadata). supply-chain-policy.md: no OSV Puppet ecosystem and no purl `puppet` type (sixth format without advisory coverage); refusals hold (single Forge per client, 403 fails the run). proxy-cache.md: the Forge has no explicit security signal; commercial gating is PER MODULE (12 of 221 puppetlabs modules premium or login_required answer 401 on every release file since 2024-05-07, metadata stays anonymous): a proxy needs a Forge API key, answers an explicit 403 without one, and never serves such modules from an anonymously readable repository. Rollback visibility: r10k :latest sees it; `puppet module upgrade` and `install` do not without --force.
26. From hackage.md (d31e54b). THIRD FORMAT CONFIRMING THE POINTER-SCOPED FRESHNESS MECHANISM (Debian item 20, Conan item 22): TUF clients (cabal 3.8 and 3.16, Stack 2.9 and 3.11) FAIL HARD on old metadata served as stored ("Version of timestamp.json is less than the previous version") and ADOPT the same content re-signed at a higher version. Timestamp and snapshot are pointer-scoped with a per-pointer version counter; every pointer move re-signs at the next version. data-model.md: one general mechanism for all three (pointer-held signed or dated documents, monotonic per pointer). data-model.md and storage-and-gc.md: documents must be able to declare blob digests that GC marks through (the index is stored as segments whose blobs the document declares: fourth root's reach extended). auth.md: X-ApiKey as a token presentation form in AC31; the 401 must carry WWW-Authenticate: Basic for cabal 3.16 to send credentials (cabal 3.8 sends none on downloads and uploads only with Digest). signing-service.md: the ten-item TUF requirement list in hackage.md Design (root held by the service by default with an operator-held offline option; root rotation keeps old keys and is signed by both sets; hosted and virtual repositories sign an EMPTY mirrors.json because both cabal lines retry a refused request on every signed mirror). artifact-verification.md: hackage-security chain verification and a "verified by repository chain" verdict. upstream-adapters.md: incremental upstream index fetches by byte range, chunked storage reused across revisions, redirects followed inside the adapter. management-api.md: revise, set preferred-versions, delete (rebuilds the index without it, retires the version), key rotation, offline root. conformance-harness.md: seeding through `state` goes through the signing and index service; case scripts read the root key ids from the server. INDEX TRAP: the index is append-only and clients fetch it by byte range assuming the new compressed file extends the old one; recompressing makes every client fail twice then download everything; append one gzip member per write, never recompress. UPSTREAM BUGS (not ours, worth reporting upstream): both cabal lines' --ignore-expiry default is inverted in source, so expiry is never checked by default; Stack 2.9.1's built-in root key ids fail against live Hackage's current root. supply-chain-policy.md: OSV covers Hackage (32 HSEC advisories; purl `hackage` type); remote repositories serve Hackage's own mirrors.json, so their refusals hold only without other egress.
27. From luarocks.md (2abd54b). SECURITY: the manifest is Lua the client executes, and the registry must never run Lua: 3.8.0 on Lua 5.1 executed a bytecode manifest (captured), make-manifest hung on a looping rockspec (captured), and luarocks.org reportedly suffered remote code execution in September 2026 from evaluating uploaded rockspecs (per the agent's research; bytecode rejection added 2026-09-26; verify before citing externally). Adopted: rockspecs parsed and constant-folded, never executed, enforced by an architecture test forbidding any interpreter or cgo (accepts 388 of 400 sampled rockspecs); manifests rendered from a stored record set by a data-only serializer; upstream Lua never relayed. auth.md: a token presentation form as the /api/1/{key}/ URL path segment on upload routes, with AC27's plaintext refusal and AC7's redaction extended to it; a LuaRocks client row (Basic only after a challenge via wget from userinfo or .netrc; preemptive via curl). data-model.md: a monotonic per-pointer change time, compatible with conan's repoint time (FOURTH format after debian, conan, hackage: a 304 hid a rollback, so Last-Modified must be the serving pointer's change time, strictly increasing). signing-service.md: the record set, the renderer with data-only serializer (36 manifest documents rendered on request, memoised), pointer-derived Last-Modified, the virtual merge. management-api.md: remove version, package, single rock. upstream-adapters.md: rocks-server adapter preferring JSON manifests, revalidating with If-Modified-Since, following /dev/ and namespace trees. proxy-cache.md: add luarocks to the completion-only verifier request. conformance-harness.md: redaction covers the key path segment and URL userinfo; client images built from pinned bases (no official image). supply-chain-policy.md: no OSV LuaRocks ecosystem (seventh format); clients do not verify by default (a tampered rock installed and ran its payload); a refused manifest falls through to the next server group and luarocks.org stays behind any --server; the client unions servers and takes the highest version (dependency confusion, captured). artifact-verification.md: an optional OpenPGP .asc verdict source.
28. From cpan.md (c86fc0a). CROSS-CUTTING TRANSPORT CONSTRAINT: many clients print only the HTTP reason phrase on a refusal, never the body (confirmed for Maven, Gradle, R, renv, Chef, CPAN clients and others), and HTTP/2 HAS NO REASON PHRASE. Refusal-bearing routes of those formats must be served over HTTP/1.1, or refusals reach users as bare status codes. Record in supply-chain-policy.md (per-format refusal rendering) and in deployment.md (to author: HTTP version per route or per format; affects reverse proxies and TLS terminators). SECURITY: cpanm 1.7044 prints "Verified OK!" after a BAD signature and for an unsigned file; cpm and Carton install swapped bytes silently; cpanm verifies only with --verify and silently skips when Module::Signature is missing; CPAN.pm checks CHECKSUMS SHA-256 by default. OrePAN2 let a second author's distribution take over Acme::Base and cpanm installed it: indexing permissions (first-come per package, owned by distribution, conflicting publish 409, transfer administrative) must be enforced by us. storage-and-gc.md: no digest check on read, so a storage alteration reaches cpm, Carton and cpanm-without---verify users unchecked; consider serve-time verification. FIFTH format needing pointer-scoped freshness (per-pointer Last-Modified with exact-match 304; cpm and Carton revalidate by If-Modified-Since). data-model.md and storage-and-gc.md: reuse hackage's declared-blob list for CHECKSUMS per author directory. supply-chain-policy.md: OSV has no CPAN ecosystem (eighth format); the separate CPANSA feed (2,117 advisories) is evidence for revisiting the single-feed OSV decision; every CPAN client has a route back to public CPAN (Carton hard-codes cpan.metacpan.org and backpan; cpanm consults cpanmetadb and MetaCPAN; cpm never reads the mirror index by default; CPAN.pm 2.38 pushy_https ignores the mirror list), so refusals and private namespaces hold only with specific client settings or blocked egress. conformance-harness.md: stand-ins for five public hostnames on the case network under the harness CA; harness-built digest-pinned images for cpm, Carton and cpan-upload; seed path through the signing service. signing-service.md: CPAN::Checksums-compatible cleartext signing per author directory, key per repository, announced rotation. artifact-verification.md: OpenPGP cleartext verification including subkeys and expiry (PAUSE's exported key has signing subkeys that expired 2026-07-02; newer CHECKSUMS are signed by a subkey only public keyservers carry, so CPAN.pm check_sigs with the exported key refused a live package). upstream-adapters.md: identity-only Accept-Encoding (www.cpan.org gzips CHECKSUMS on request). management-api.md: author records, ownership transfer, a "not indexed" report on publish; a cpan-upload binding onto publish.
29. From opam.md (5efda96). REFUSAL VIA REWRITE: opam silently falls back from the repository cache to the opam file's source URL and mirrors, Software Heritage, its own cache and a global archive-mirrors (which 2.2+ try BEFORE ours; the official ocaml/opam images configure opam.ocaml.org's cache globally). An opam file served as uploaded can refuse nothing. Adopted: rewrite every source URL to the registry's own archive route, remove mirrors and swhid, keep the relative `archive-mirrors: "cache"` (the only path by which a private repository's credential reaches the archive; the absolute rewritten URL is fetched without credentials, captured 401). This is a general pattern worth naming in supply-chain-policy.md: where a client falls back to URLs inside the metadata, the registry must own those URLs. Build commands: never evaluate an opam file; parse only, architecture test (luarocks precedent). NOT needing pointer-scoped freshness: opam downloads the whole index on every update with no conditional request, so rollbacks are visible (contrast debian, conan, hackage, luarocks, cpan). auth.md: opam row (Basic pre-emptively from repository-URL userinfo; the client prints the token and stores it in plaintext). proxy-cache.md: a declared digest set in md5, sha256 and sha512, and an ordered list of candidate sources with an off-origin host allowlist (extends composer's allowlist request; default allowlist empty). upstream-adapters.md: opam HTTP-repository adapter (If-None-Match on the index, read archive-mirrors, ignore redirect: a `redirect` in the upstream repo file is permanent on the client, captured, so the repo file is never relayed). supply-chain-policy.md: OSV covers opam (29 OSEC advisories over 18 packages, no MAL) but needs opam version ordering for ECOSYSTEM ranges (`~` sorts before everything); clients verify against every checksum in the opam file, but those come from our unsigned index, so the registry is the root of trust. management-api.md: publish (opam file, files/ entries, archives; the FIRST format where publish itself has no client trigger), revise with archives unchanged, remove version or package. signing-service.md: seven items in the spec (deterministic whole-gzip index rendered from a record set; unsigned). Management-surfaces analysis: an opam row. data-model.md: no pointer change-time needed for opam.
30. From homebrew.md (81e5062). FRESHNESS MECHANISM REFINEMENT: curl's --time-cond discards any 200 whose Last-Modified is not newer than brew's own clock at its last fetch; a rollback was masked under both "not later" and "exact match" 304 rules and became visible only when the served Last-Modified moved FORWARD. So the general pointer/cache-scoped freshness mechanism (items 20, 22, 26, 27, 28) must be monotonic and forward-moving; exact-match 304 (cpan's fix) is insufficient for curl-based clients. proxy-cache.md: a cache's served Last-Modified is cache-scoped, never the upstream's, and never a 200 older than the request's If-Modified-Since (may also affect CPAN and LuaRocks remotes). HOSTED API IMPOSSIBLE: brew accepts only JWS documents (PS512, b64:false) signed under kid homebrew-1 with a key compiled into the client, no setting to add a key; hosted repositories serve flat bottles only, at a tap's root_url; proxies serve the JWS documents unmodified; signing-service.md is asked for NOTHING by this format. artifact-verification.md: a PS512 JWS verifier; a bottle is "verified" when a verified API document names its digest. auth.md: brew never follows an auth challenge and sends a placeholder `Bearer QQ==` on bottle-manifest requests (7.0.6 with HOMEBREW_BOTTLE_DOMAIN), which auth AC12 turns into a 401; no special case, the recipe sets a real token or none. upstream-adapters.md: ghcr.io's per-scope anonymous token exchange (it currently accepts any bearer value), the 307 cross-host redirect to pkg-containers.githubusercontent.com without Authorization, identity encoding, a post-receipt verifier hook. oci.md: covers only a manifest-by-tag and blob-by-digest subset of what brew reaches; add a remote covering a whole upstream namespace. management-api.md: bottle publish (archive plus its `brew bottle --json` record) and delete (no yank). supply-chain-policy.md: decide advisory coverage from the feed's own ecosystem list (osv.dev recognises "Homebrew" but serves no records; Homebrew's own OSV-format database has 13,023 records); fallback: the API and bottle domains fall back on any failure; only HOMEBREW_ARTIFACT_DOMAIN with ..._NO_FALLBACK on 7.x keeps a bottle refusal without egress control (the documented recipe); brew prints only `curl: (22) ... 403`, so nothing forces HTTP/1.1. conformance-harness.md: the API stand-in serves a recorded snapshot of genuinely Homebrew-signed documents; case-network names for ghcr.io, pkg-containers.githubusercontent.com, formulae.brew.sh, raw.githubusercontent.com and a source host. Taps (Git) out of scope (cargo, julia precedent).
31. From openvsx.md (05cf090). Editors cannot keep installs inside the registry: they retry at fallbackAssetUri (captured going to another host) and ovsx follows any files.download URL and cross-host redirect, so every served document is regenerated from records, never relayed (opam's rewrite rule, item 29, generalised). VSCodium and code-server install a tampered package with exit 0 and never fetch the signature; Microsoft VS Code rejects Open VSX signatures and unsigned packages alike unless extensions.verifySignature is off. Editors drop URL credentials and read a Basic challenge as "not found": private reads use a pull-only path-segment token (-/t/{token}). A refused download is written out as the package and fails as a corrupt zip; the only refusal an editor explains is the control document's `malicious` list, so whole-extension condemnations go into a registry-served control document. auth.md: Open VSX client row; write credentials accepted as the `token` query parameter (released ovsx sends only this), X-OpenVSX-Token and Bearer, on the four write routes only; pull-only path-segment token on read routes; redaction and plaintext refusal for both. Namespace ownership maps onto {namespace}/** pattern scopes with a namespace-bound publish base (-/ns/{ns}). management-api.md: removal taking (version, target) lists; declare and withdraw verified-namespace (admin only). signing-service.md: pure Ed25519 (not Ed25519ph) detached signatures over a stored blob inside the publish; bounded memory to ~250 MiB (the reference needed a 2 GB heap, its issue #1450); one rotatable key per repository, every public key retrievable by digest. artifact-verification.md: the raw Ed25519 envelope, per-remote key pinning, verdict computed after the verified commit. proxy-cache.md: a declared digest obtained through a prior metadata request (the upstream .sha256); Open VSX removal-table rows; storage-host allowlist. upstream-adapters.md: Open VSX adapter with storage-host allowlist and control-document fetching (only the malicious list is used, as a security signal). supply-chain-policy.md: OSV `VSCode` ecosystem (21 advisories, all MAL, mostly under VSCode:https://open-vsx.org, case-sensitive names): match every variant case-insensitively with semver ordering; purl type vscode-extension. credential-management.md: ovsx trusted publishing (OIDC token exchange). catalogue.md: VSCodium, code-server, VS Code and ovsx in the client-reach column. Client quirks: ovsx 1.2.0 refuses unpublish if /api/version reports below 1.2.0 (so we report v1.3.0); --skip-duplicate matches the error text "is already published."; gallery filter type 8 means two things; editors prefer the newest version over the most specific target platform. No pointer-scoped freshness needed (rollback visible at next query).
32. From arch.md (2867914). SEVENTH FORMAT CONFIRMING FORWARD-MOVING POINTER/CACHE-SCOPED FRESHNESS: the db and its .sig are separate downloads with no shared version; pacman 7.1 always re-downloads .db.sig but checks .db by If-Modified-Since, so a db changed without a forward-moving Last-Modified is paired with the new signature and the sync fails once; pacman 6.0.2 checks both conditionally and silently keeps the newer db forever. Adopted: per-pointer forward-moving Last-Modified shared by a db and its signature, 304 only on exact match. proxy-cache.md: a remote never adopts an older upstream revision and serves its own cache-scoped Last-Modified; a db and its signature adopted together as one revision. signing-service.md: databases and package signatures (the repository key signs a detached .sig for every hosted package, leaving bytes unchanged; the publisher's signature becomes a policy verdict); ONE signature per .sig file, never two (every signature in a .sig must verify with a trusted key, which rules out the dual-signature rotation apk and apt allow): rotation is announce, switch in one write, retire; no host extended attributes in the database tar; one OpenPGP key per repository, RSA 4096 or Ed25519, at signing-key.asc behind normal read auth. auth.md: pacman row (Basic only after a 401; root's .netrc fails under Arch 7.1's DownloadUser privilege drop; a root-only included Server line works on all three clients). artifact-verification.md: OpenPGP entry where every signature must verify; package checked against its database record. upstream-adapters.md: return the upstream's Last-Modified with each revision. conformance-harness.md: seed path calls the signing service; pacman cases write their own pacman.conf, set up the keyring (pacman-key --init, --add, --lsign-key) and install the CA with trust anchor. supply-chain-policy.md: OSV has no Arch ecosystem; Arch's own tracker (2,444 records, keyed by package base) is a candidate second advisory source (with CPAN's CPANSA, evidence against the single-feed decision); under the shipped DatabaseOptional a .db.sig answering 403 or 404 is silently accepted, so the client recipe requires DatabaseRequired; a refused database fails the whole command (unlike dnf, zypper and apk); pacman builds its own error message, so HTTP/2 is fine. management-api.md: batch publish per database with an optional publisher .sig; the three rotation phases. catalogue.md: Manjaro in the Arch client column once proven.

## From artifact-verification.md authoring (2026-09-27, issue #46)
Addresses supply-chain fold item 3, format-management fold item 2, Open items 9, 21, 27, 31 and theme 4.
1. data-model.md, "Records that are not mark roots": add "Verification records: verdicts keyed (repository, blob digest, scheme), trust sets and revisions, superseded marks and the re-evaluation queue (artifact-verification.md)"; not a root, since a verdict must outlive the blob it explains and a pinned blob would make refused content uncollectable; AC34's proof extends to them.
2. storage-and-gc.md, "Addressing" (or a new read-path section) plus a new AC: the CAS read path verifies the blob digest while streaming and aborts with an operator alert on mismatch, on every read, under a read-path benchmark budget (artifact-verification resolved Q5; the only integrity check for conan, chef, luarocks, cpm/Carton and the editors).
3. proxy-cache.md, "Integrity of fetched content": add the completion-only fetch mode with a post-receipt verifier hook (theme 7); verdicts are computed from the CAS blob after commit inside that hook and never gate the commit or delay the initiating client; integrity calls (JWS, tree hash, apk segment, TUF chain, SHA256SUMS) in the hook may refuse the commit.
4. conformance-harness.md, "The `setup` vocabulary" and AC17's not-yet-landed list: add a `trust` key (a repository's trust set verbatim: keys, roots, identity policies, a sigstore-root file; owner artifact-verification.md), applied through the seed path.
5. management-api.md, repository administration and endpoint table: `GET/PUT/DELETE /api/v1/repositories/{name}/trust` (admin; PUT is one revision), `POST .../trust/import`, `GET /api/v1/repositories/{name}/verdicts/{digest}` plus a listing filtered by `state`/`scheme`; the Ansible `attach` row reads "attach signature (verified over MANIFEST.json before storage, refused `validation`)" under `push`. No new problem type.
6. format-handler-interface.md, "The pinned method set" Deps paragraph: Deps also carries the `Verifier` consumer interface (`Check`, `Verify`) declared beside it; handlers never import `internal/verify` (`internal/format/verify_boundary_test.go`). Method set unchanged.
7. supply-chain-policy.md, "Signature and attestation state is a consumed verdict" and AC15: the producer exists; a verified verdict carries a chain (`publisher` or `repository-chain`) and a `revocation` field, so a rule may require a publisher identity and be strict on revocation; a verdict under a superseded trust revision reads as absent.
8. pypi.md, Scope, "The upload path", AC14: lift the refusal to "attestations verified synchronously before commit against the repository's identity policy; valid accepted, stored as a version file and served as PEP 740 provenance in both serializations with api-version 1.3; tampered or off-policy refused, nothing committed; with no identity policy configured, refused as today"; add the proxied re-host rule (artifact-verification AC8).
9. ansible-collections.md, "Signatures", AC11, Q3/Q7 records: hosted signatures via management-api `attach`, verified before storage; served entry `{signature, pubkey_fingerprint, signing_service: null, pulp_created}`; AC11 becomes "a real ansible-galaxy requiring one valid signature installs the attached-and-verified collection and refuses a tampered one; proxied signatures pass through with a verdict".
10. npm.md, Scope and a new Design section: proxied `dist.signatures` verified by keyid against pinned upstream keys; `dist.attestations` verified and re-hosted at `/-/npm/v1/attestations/{name}@{version}`; hosted `_attestations` verified before commit; hosted `dist.signatures` and keys stay signing-service.md's; a new AC mirroring artifact-verification AC20.
11. nuget.md, "Signing and provenance": revocation answered (cached/required/off, status on the verdict); an untrusted root is failed/untrusted-key; owners are part of the repository-signature identity.
12. credential-management.md, "OIDC exchange": a robot's trust policy is also an identity source for verification identity policies; an exchanged token's upload must attest under the same robot (artifact-verification AC28); the claim-to-certificate-identity mapping lives beside the issuer list.
13. signing-service.md (to author): it produces (Galaxy server-side signing if ever, NuGet countersign before CAS commit, npm keys and signatures, Hex/Debian/RPM/apk/Arch index signatures) and may hand attachments through the verified `attach` path; verifying anything is artifact-verification's.
14. oci.md, Scope "Out of scope for v1 ... signature verification enforcement": cite artifact-verification's Cosign entry (AC6); add tag-convention discovery (`sha256-<hex>.sig`/`.att`) beside the referrers query; a case `conformance/oci/cosign_test.go`.
15. project-charter.md, step 4b: only the core and the Sigstore entry land at 4b; per-format entries are built with their formats and charged to them under the cost procedure.
16. catalogue.md and the conformance matrix: a verification column, `none` for chef, composer, cran, debian, go-modules, hex, opam, pub, cargo, conan, generic (artifact-verification AC24).

## From signing-service.md authoring (2026-09-27, issue #47)
Addresses replication item 6, Open items 9, 11, 12, 14-32, theme 1 (the freshness split), management-api item 14 and artifact-verification item 13.
OWNER NOTE: signing-service Q2 places signatures in records outside snapshot content, which contradicts the "one write re-signs every document" wording in hex, arch, rpm and alpine (item 9 below).
1. data-model.md, entity table, "Records that are not mark roots", AC34: add `Signature` (repository, body digest, key id, profile, bytes, created; not content, not a root, pruned with its body), `PointerDocument` (pointer, document key, inline bytes or CAS digest, produced-at, counter value; outside snapshot content), `SigningKey` (repository ref, purpose, algorithm, state announced|active|retired, backend kind and reference, public forms, created), and a per-pointer freshness record on `Pointer` (`moved_at` = later of transition time and previous+1s; generation counter incremented per transition and re-sign). Qualify AC22 (content byte-identical across environments; pointer documents and freshness signals are not). Extend the CAS-backed document model with a declared blob-digest list (hackage segments, cpan manifest). Expose a pre-commit hook on the write transaction for the index runtime. (signing-service AC6, AC10, AC27; theme 1)
2. storage-and-gc.md, fourth root paragraph and AC16: the current-document half also covers `PointerDocument` blobs, virtual merged documents, and marks through declared blob-digest lists (items 20, 26).
3. proxy-cache.md, freshness: own the cache-scoped forward-moving `Last-Modified` for remotes, never adopt an older upstream revision, adopt db+sig as one revision (items 30, 32); a remote's documents are never signed by this registry.
4. management-api.md, endpoint table, `configure` row, AC25: `POST/GET /api/v1/repositories/{name}/signing-keys`, `POST .../signing-keys/{id}/activate|retire`, `POST .../signing-keys/import`, `POST .../signing-keys/{id}/document` (external submit), all `configure`, admin; `configure` args naming the signing key set dispatch to `internal/signing`'s `Apply`, with the handler's generator run in the same transaction.
5. replication.md, read surface, takeover preconditions, Design: `Signature` and `PointerDocument` records replicate with the pointer set; a linked follower serves verbatim and signs nothing; takeover refused unless every active key resolves on the follower; "future signing-service spec" wording (~l.769, ~l.842) becomes a citation.
6. conformance-harness.md, `repositories` setup entry: optional `signing` sub-entry (fixture private key file or `generate`); `state` seeding yields signed documents via the write-path hook with no seed-side code.

9. hex.md item 4, arch.md item 6, rpm.md item 6, alpine.md item 5 ("What the signing and index service must provide"): "rotation as one write that re-signs every stored document" -> "one atomic batch of signature records, no snapshot"; hex and alpine: the signature is a record framed onto the body at serve time (protobuf `Signed`, prepended tar segment).
10. debian.md item 6 and hex.md item 5: re-cite verification to artifact-verification.md's `openpgp` / `raw` scheme.
11. cpan.md, arch.md, luarocks.md, hackage.md, conan.md ("Pointers, rollback and freshness"): cite data-model.md's per-pointer freshness record instead of their own; all 22 consumer specs: drop "(to be authored in the spec loop)" and cite the generator contract (`Indexer`, `internal/format/<name>/index`).
12. helm.md, index document section and Context precondition: Helm consumes the service (signing-service was Q1).
13. npm.md, Design: hosted `dist.signatures` and `/-/npm/v1/keys`, when adopted, are this service's ECDSA P-256 profile and keys document (Phase 5).
14. project-charter.md step 7 / AC12: name Maven, Debian, RPM and the other signed consumers beside Helm; Maven is the first, unsigned consumer inside the step.
15. async-operations.md (to author): execution of the virtual merge (enqueue on member commit, coalescing window, atomic swap, staleness bound as fixed in signing-service) and the cadence re-sign scheduler. deployment.md (to author): the 15 `signing.*`/`index.*` keys, master-key and PIN handling, SoftHSM2 recipe, replicated-repository key recipe. observability.md (to author): key expiry gauge, signing latency, lock wait, merge staleness.

## From upstream-adapters.md authoring (2026-09-27, issue #48)
Addresses Open items 7, 11, 13, every line naming upstream-adapters.md, and the adapter halves of themes 2 and 7.
1. proxy-cache.md, "Integrity of fetched content" plus a new "The adapter seam" paragraph: fetch-and-cache passes the handler's upstream location and `upstream.Options` to a one-method `Fetcher` (satisfied by `*upstream.Router`); the completion-only mode and verifier hook are proxy-cache's (theme 7, artifact-verification item 3) and rely on the adapter's `ErrTruncated`/`ErrStalled`; AC9 consumes `upstream.RateLimitError` by `errors.As`; the stall timeout travels in `Options`.
2. proxy-cache.md, Scope "Preconfigured upstreams", AC15, AC19, the was-Q14 record: add api.nuget.org and repo.maven.apache.org via the extension mechanism; each entry's definition lives in `internal/upstream/preconfigured` and upstream-adapters AC24 holds the tables equal.
3. proxy-cache.md, Scope "Upstream credentials" and AC6: cite upstream-adapters.md for credential kinds and the redactor, management-api.md for administration; AC6's integration case runs through `internal/upstream`'s redactor.
4. data-model.md, `Upstream` row and "Records that are not mark roots": extend `Upstream` with `adapter` (default `https`), `hosts` ({host, role none|root|own(credential ref)}), `tls` {ca_bundle ref, client cert/key ref, insecure_skip_verify, allow_http}, `limits` {concurrency default 10, cooldown_cap default 1h}, `http2`; add `UpstreamCredential` (name, kind, material encrypted under the instance master key, created, rotated_at, last_used). Neither is a mark root.
5. management-api.md, "Repository administration" and the closed problem-type list: call `upstream.Validate` on remote create and PATCH; add problem type `upstream-invalid`; the upstream-credential resource carries `kind` from upstream-adapters' table with per-kind fields.
6. format-handler-interface.md, resolved adapter axis (was Q4): ownership now upstream-adapters.md; Deps: fetch-and-cache carries `upstream.Options` (method set unchanged); handlers never import `internal/upstream` (upstream-adapters AC29).
7. conformance-harness.md, `upstreams` setup key: an entry may declare `adapter`, `credential` (kind plus fixture material) and `hosts` (stand-ins for allowlisted off-origin hosts, e.g. a codeload stand-in); the redaction rule (AC13) extends to presigned query strings (`X-Amz-*`, `X-Goog-Signature`) and path tokens.
8. artifact-verification.md, Scope/Context: per-upstream trust anchors requested of upstream-adapters by debian (keyring), hex (public key), openvsx (pinned keys) and rpm (metalink hash) are the remote repository's trust set.
9. auth.md AC10: upstream credential kinds (cloud IAM material, token exchange, path tokens) and the redactor are auth-adjacent surface for the external review.
10. oci.md Phase 4: cite `internal/upstream/distribution` and the Docker Hub profile; add `conformance/oci/upstream_dockerhub_test.go`; capture Docker Hub's blob-redirect CDN hosts at the proxied phase to fill the profile's allowlist.
11. composer.md, "The proxied path" and resolved dist-host decision: the GitHub credential is an allowlist entry `api.github.com` with role `own`; default dist hosts are role `none`; the 60/h limit surfaces as `RateLimitError` from GitHub's 403 with `x-ratelimit-remaining: 0`; add `conformance/composer/upstream_dist_host_test.go` and `audit_forward_test.go`.
12. One sentence each in chef, conan, luarocks, openvsx, opam, hackage, swift, vagrant, puppet, terraform, julia, homebrew, alpine, arch, rpm, cpan, cran, conda, hex, debian, go-modules: drop "(to be authored in the spec loop)"; the transport half of their requested "adapter" is the `https`/`distribution` adapter under the upstream's allowlist and credential role; the protocol half stays in the handler's proxied-path derivation.
13. supply-chain-policy.md, theme 2 refusal conditions: cite upstream-adapters AC8 (no upstream `Location` reaches a client) and AC6 (no credential follows metadata to another host).
14. observability.md (to author): per-upstream request counters by outcome, rate-limit-remaining gauge, cool-down state, token-exchange failures. deployment.md (to author): `upstream.*` defaults (concurrency 10, cooldown cap 1h, connect timeout 10s, User-Agent, `HTTPS_PROXY`/`NO_PROXY` honoured once instance-wide).

## From async-operations.md authoring (2026-09-27, issue #49)
Addresses format-management item 3's reconciliation note, management-api items 2 and 14, signing-service item 15, and write-triggered-services-prototype question 5 (grace).
OWNER NOTE: async-operations Q9 moves the queue core from charter step 6a to the start of step 4b (verification and policy consume it); the 6a placement was a reconciliation act, not an owner decision. Also, like `internal/retention`, `internal/async` is a shared package outside the storage/proxy/auth trio: record it in CLAUDE.md if that list is meant to be exhaustive.
1. data-model.md, entity table, a new subsection, "Records that are not mark roots", AC34: add `Job` (id, kind, opaque args doc, state pending|running|completed|failed|cancelled, run_at, attempts, max_attempts, created/finished, last_error, lease_owner, lease_expires_at, lease_token, coalesce_key, exclusive_key, optional operation ref, optional repository ref, checkpoint doc; partial unique indexes on coalesce_key where pending and exclusive_key where running; not content, not a root, pruned after `async.job_retention`, never before its Operation) and `Schedule` (name, kind, args, interval or next_run_at, last_run_at, last_result, enabled).
2. data-model.md, "Operations" and AC32: add `cancelled` as a third terminal state; a pending operation's bytes are protected by the job-held grace (async-operations was Q4), replacing the "prototype's question 5" wording.
3. management-api.md, deferred operations and AC16: admit `cancelled`; add `POST /api/v1/operations/{id}/cancel` (originating authorization or admin), `GET /api/v1/system/jobs` (admin; filter kind/state/repository), `POST /api/v1/system/jobs/{id}/cancel`, `POST/DELETE /api/v1/system/jobs/kinds/{kind}/pause` (admin), each with an audit line and OpenAPI presence; the Scope line on workers, retries and cancellation cites async-operations.md; `operation-outstanding` means "job not terminal".
4. storage-and-gc.md, upload-session grace paragraph, property generator, near AC20: an unfinished `Job` naming a repository holds that repository's grace open exactly as an unexpired upload session does; the property op set gains "queued/retrying job" and its terminal transition; sweep, orphan scan and prune are enqueued by the async scheduler (mechanics and advisory lock unchanged).
5. signing-service.md, "Package shape" (drop "except the cadence scheduler": the cadence is a `Schedule` on internal/async), "Virtual merges" execution bullet, AC19/AC22 Test Plan rows (production runtime is internal/async; fixture runner only until step 4b).
6. artifact-verification.md, "Re-evaluation when a trust set changes", config table, AC3: the worker is the `verify.reevaluate` kind with a checkpoint per page; `verify.workers` becomes `async.kind_limits[verify.reevaluate]` (default 4); TUF and revocation refreshes are `Schedule`s.
7. supply-chain-policy.md, "Scanning is asynchronous" and "The advisory feed": scans are `policy.scan` jobs (coalesce key `scan:{digest}`, per-kind retry bound feeding AC6's alert); feed sync is a `Schedule` disabled under offline mode.
8. replication.md, sync/transfer design and AC3: sync runs as `replication.sync` jobs per link with a checkpoint per completed snapshot; resume is the queue's rescue path.
9. generic.md, resolved was-Q11 and the `internal/retention` phase: the pass is the `retention.pass` kind scheduled per repository with rules, exclusive key the repository.
10. project-charter.md, steps 4b and 6a, AC12, Phases 3 and 5: the queue core (async-operations Phases 1-3) is the first item of step 4b after the re-open finding; step 6a becomes "the deferred management operation on the async runner, then Ansible collections".
11. write-triggered-services-prototype.md, AC8 Test Plan row and Phase 4: the hold is the admin pause of the `manage.apply` kind; the disposable runner mirrors the claim/lease/fence shape; AC11's expected answer is the grace hold.
12. format-handler-interface.md, "The scheduled re-open": the runner reaches the handler only through `Operator.Apply` (prototype question 4, no pinned method); the goroutine/ticker architecture test generalises prototype AC12 to every package.
13. conformance-harness.md, "What sibling specs already require": the Galaxy deferred case's `script` calls the pause/resume admin routes; no new setup key.
14. observability.md (to author): queue depth and oldest-pending age per kind, lease expiries, retries, permanent failures, scheduler leadership, merge-staleness breaches; alerts on failed job, staleness breach, schedule overdue by 2x period. deployment.md (to author): the 11 `async.*` keys and the `workers: 0` web-replica recipe.

## From repository-lifecycle.md authoring (2026-09-27, issue #50)
Addresses Open item 12, theme 9, management-api items 3 and 4, async-operations item 4.
OWNER NOTE: `internal/repository` is another shared package outside the storage/proxy/auth trio, like `internal/retention` and `internal/async`.
1. data-model.md, `Repository` row and "Records that are not mark roots": add `state` (active|read_only|deleted), `deleted_at`, `deleted_by`, `effective_retention_override` (for `reclaim: now`); identity is the primary key (128-bit random, `rep_` rendering); the unique index on `name` becomes partial `WHERE state <> 'deleted'`; the tombstone row is a never-removed non-root.
2. data-model.md, "Snapshots, pointers and what counts as a write": deleting a `local` is a completed write producing a final empty checkpoint snapshot (the retention-pass shape); `remote` and `virtual` also get the initial empty snapshot and default pointer at creation.
3. storage-and-gc.md, GC design and property op set (AC4/AC17/AC18): add create, delete local with and without `reclaim`, delete remote, detach, freeze, thaw, rename to `internal/storage/gc_property_test.go`; AC15's scan includes `internal/repository`; the pruner drops a deleted repository's final snapshot and default pointer and does the tombstone cleanup in its cycle; retention passes are suspended on `read_only` while pruning continues.
4. storage-and-gc.md, write-transaction opener: the sole write-transaction constructor calls `repository.Writable` (lifecycle AC9); replication's link-refusal architecture test generalises into it.
5. management-api.md, "Repository administration", problem types, endpoint table, AC20: problem types `in-use` (409), `read-only` (405), `capability-unsupported` (422); delete fields `confirm` (identity, required) and `detach`; `POST .../repositories/{name}/freeze|thaw` and `.../rename`; a `lifecycle` administration kind; `GET /api/v1/repositories/deleted` (admin); responses carry the identity; AC20 cites lifecycle for semantics; AC21's credential delete refused `in-use` while referenced.
6. format-handler-interface.md, `Capabilities()` row and AC13: add `Virtual` and `Rename` (supported|unsupported); `internal/format/arch_test.go` asserts no import between handlers and `internal/repository` either way; `configure` may carry `{"rename": {"from","to"}}`.
7. replication.md, `ReplicationLink` status, Design, AC11: terminal status `ended` with reason (`deleted`, `takeover`); the link's leader repository name is updatable (leader rename or deletion surfaces as `failed` naming `not-found`); a `read_only` repository stays read-only after takeover; cite `repository.Writable` as the shared predicate.
8. credential-management.md, AC20 and listing: a deleted repository's name renders from the tombstone; grants, not credentials, are deleted in the deletion transaction.
9. signing-service.md, key state and custody: deletion retires every key of the repository in the deletion transaction; public forms retrievable by digest until tombstone time; private material destroyed at tombstone time; the first virtual merge is enqueued at virtual creation.
10. artifact-verification.md, trust sets: trust set revisions dropped at tombstone time, verdicts never; rename changes nothing.
11. supply-chain-policy.md, "Policy is per repository": rules dropped at tombstone time; condemnation and refusal records never.
12. async-operations.md, cancellation and `Job`: deletion cancels every pending job naming the repository in its transaction and cooperatively cancels running ones; a job observing a deleted repository ends itself; repository-scoped `Schedule`s are disabled; the grace hold stands until terminal.
13. upstream-adapters.md, `UpstreamCredential`: deleting a credential referenced by an `Upstream` or `ReplicationLink` is refused `in-use`; changing a remote's upstream keeps cached references and resets `RemoteFile.last-checked`.
14. proxy-cache.md, eviction and freshness: a `read_only` remote does no fetch, revalidation or eviction and serves cache-only with a frozen cache-scoped `Last-Modified`; remote deletion ends cached references in eviction's shape.
15. conformance-harness.md, `repositories` entry and per-kind case rule: an entry may declare `read_only`, and a deleted repository a later entry recreates under the same name; every format carries `rename_test.go`; generic and oci carry `lifecycle_test.go`, `readonly_test.go`, `readonly_remote_test.go`, `virtual_detach_test.go`; the matrix renders `Virtual: unsupported` as exempt.
16. hex.md, resolved was-Q1 accepted cost: cite `Capabilities()` `Virtual: unsupported` and `Rename: unsupported`; catalogue.md and the matrix: a virtual column driven by the capability.
17. project-charter.md step 2: repository-lifecycle Phases 1-2 are the repository half of the management surface core.
18. generic.md, "Overwrite and immutability": immutability is distinct from `read_only`; both may hold.

OWNER NOTE: `internal/telemetry` is another shared package outside the storage/proxy/auth trio. Observability Q3 makes the audit stream best-effort (a sink failure alerts but never fails the request); reversible if compliance needs write-through.

4. management-api.md, wire conventions and "Audit": X-Request-Id validation (1-128 bytes `[A-Za-z0-9._-]`, else replaced) and echo are the middleware's on every listener (observability AC14); audit emission is `telemetry.Auditor.Emit` with `manage.*` events registered; AC23's test uses `telemetry.NewTestRecorder`.
5. credential-management.md, AC4/AC18 Test Plan rows: scan through `telemetry.NewTestRecorder` (metrics, spans, logs, audit); `credential.*` events and `credential`/`owner` extension attributes registered; gauge `stackweaver_registry_credentials{state,owner_kind}`.
6. auth.md, AC7 Test Plan (`internal/auth/leak_test.go`): the scan also covers span attributes and audit records for every AC31 form; the verifier calls `telemetry.MarkSecret(ctx, value)` on every extracted credential before any other use; AC15's emission uses `telemetry.Disclose` (single call site).
7. upstream-adapters.md, "Request hygiene" and "Redaction": `traceparent`/`tracestate` never sent upstream; the adapter marks the attached credential via `MarkSecret`; a test keeps its query-parameter redaction list equal to `telemetry.RedactURL`'s; gauges `upstream_rate_limit_remaining{upstream}` and `upstream_cooldown{upstream}`.
8. async-operations.md, AC20 Test Plan and "Scheduler": metric names from observability's catalogue (`async_jobs{kind,state}`, `async_oldest_pending_age_seconds`, `async_lease_expiries_total`, `async_retries_total`, `async_jobs_total{outcome}`, `async_scheduler_leader`, `async_schedule_last_run_timestamp_seconds`/`async_schedule_period_seconds`); alerts `JobFailed`, `ScheduleOverdue`, `VirtualMergeStalenessBreach` via `telemetry.Alert`; the leader runs the state-gauge collector (observability AC7); enqueue records `trace_context`/`request_id`, job spans start with a link (AC16).
9. signing-service.md, AC14/AC22 Test Plan and "Configuration": `signing_earliest_document_expiry_timestamp_seconds{repository}`, `signing_duration_seconds{backend}`, `index_lock_wait_seconds`, `index_virtual_merge_staleness_seconds{repository}`; alerts `SigningDocumentExpiring`, `SigningFailed`, `VirtualMergeFailed`; `signing.key.*` audit events; private material typed as a LogValuer.
10. proxy-cache.md, AC10/AC13/AC14 Test Plan and resolved eviction record: `cache_referenced_bytes`/`cache_quota_bytes{repository}`; thrash `cache_refetch_after_eviction_total`/`cache_evictions_total` (alert `CacheThrash`); `CachePurgedOnSignal`; `cache_fetch_failures_total{condition}` and `FetchIntegrityFailure`; `cache.purge` audit event.
11. storage-and-gc.md, Scope reporting bullet, AC19 Test Plan, GC design: `gc_sweep_state`, `gc_sweep_duration_seconds`, `gc_last_sweep_completed_timestamp_seconds`, `gc_*_total`; `gc_pinned_out_of_window_snapshots{repository}`, `gc_pinned_out_of_window_bytes` (alert `PinnedStorageOutOfWindow`); `storage_blob_digest_mismatches_total` and `BlobDigestMismatch`; AC7's `bench_test.go` carries a `// gate:` comment under `scripts/bench-gate.sh`.
12. supply-chain-policy.md, AC5/AC6 Test Plan: `policy_refusals_total{format,condition}`, `policy_unscanned_past_bound` (alert `ArtifactUnscannedPastBound`), `policy_advisory_feed_degraded` (alert `AdvisoryFeedDegraded`); `policy.refusal`/`policy.condemnation` audit events.
13. replication.md, AC10 and Design: `replication_link_state{link,state}` one-hot, `replication_last_sync_timestamp_seconds{link}`, `replication_snapshots_behind{link}`; alerts `ReplicationLinkFailed/Reseeding/Diverged/LagHigh`; peer requests carry trace context.
14. artifact-verification.md, AC26/AC27 Test Plan: `verify_verdicts_total{scheme,state}`, alert `VerificationFailed`; `bench_test.go` carries `// gate:`.
15. repository-lifecycle.md, AC27 and audit: `repository.*` events with `repository_id` (`rep_`) in the core audit schema; gauge `repositories{format,repository_kind,state}`.
16. project-charter.md, step 2 and AC6 Test Plan: cite observability.md Phase 1; AC6's gate runs on `scripts/bench-gate.sh`.

18. deployment.md (to author): the 17 `telemetry.*` keys and defaults, the second listener `:9464` not exposed publicly, `alerts.yaml` packaging with the templated signing lead, a Grafana dashboard reading the generated catalogue, log collection and rotation, SIGHUP for the audit file.
19. .golangci.yml and `make verify` (whoever lands step 2 wiring): `sloglint` (context all, no-global all, snake keys, static msg), `forbidigo` for print families, `depguard` for the two import boundaries (observability AC1/AC2/AC8).

## From deployment.md authoring (2026-09-27, issue #52)
Addresses the deployment lines in charter item 13, credential-management 14, signing-service 15, upstream-adapters 14, async-operations 14, observability 18, themes 2 and 3, Open items 16, 18, 25, 28.
1. signing-service.md, Configuration table: replace the `signing.master_key` row with a citation of deployment.md's `security.master_key` (or `_file`); 14 keys plus the citation.
2. artifact-verification.md, Configuration table: remove `verify.workers`; the bound is `async.kind_limits` `verify.reevaluate: 4`.
3. auth.md, Design: a new "Configuration" subsection with a three-column key table: `auth.oidc.issuer`, `auth.oidc.client_id`, `auth.oidc.client_secret` (secret), `auth.oidc.redirect_url`, `auth.oidc.admin_identities` (list of {issuer, subject}), `auth.local_admin.keep` false, `auth.session.lifetime`, `auth.allow_plaintext` false (flag `--allow-plaintext-auth`).
4. proxy-cache.md, Offline mode and AC5: name the key `proxy.offline` (instance-wide, default false).
5. upstream-adapters.md, limits paragraph: a three-column key table with `upstream.default_concurrency` 10, `upstream.default_cooldown_cap` 1h, `upstream.connect_timeout` 10s; User-Agent is computed from `server.public_url`, not a key.
6. storage-and-gc.md, Design: a `gc.` key table (sweep cadence, grace period, orphan-scan cadence, defaults as its prose fixes).
7. supply-chain-policy.md: (a) a `policy.` key table (feed source URL, sync cadence); (b) Go net/http writes canonical `http.StatusText` with no custom reason-phrase API, so a format-specific phrase needs a hijacked HTTP/1.1 write in the shared refusal path; deployment guarantees only that HTTP/1.1 reaches the client (`server.http2: false`).
8. replication.md: a `replication.` key table if any instance default exists (checkpoint interval), else state it has none.
9. async-operations.md: (a) rolling upgrades: a claimed job of an unknown kind is skipped (left for a newer process), not failed; (b) take the leader lock through `internal/db/lock`'s `LockScheduler`; (c) align kind names `verify.revocation_refresh`/`verify.tuf_refresh` with artifact-verification's `verify.revocation.refresh`/`verify.sigstore.refresh`.
10. storage-and-gc.md: take the sweep lock through `internal/db/lock.LockSweep`; the consistency checker's CLI is `stackweaver-registry storage check` with `--restore-dangling` restoring from bucket versions; bucket versioning is a deployment requirement it relies on.
11. format-handler-interface.md, re-open inputs: host binding is `server.hosts` {hostname, repository} from configuration, passed at construction, reloadable on SIGHUP.
12. conformance-harness.md: the `repositories` hostname binding is written through `server.hosts`' loader; `database.schema` and `storage.s3.prefix` are the isolation keys the seed subcommand receives.
13. management-api.md, "Configuration and the CLI stance": add `migrate`, `config`, `keys`, `version` to the non-management operational subcommands beside `seed`.
14. Tooling: `scripts/check-config-keys.js` (new, deployment Phase 1) joins `make verify` and CI's docs job; every spec key table keeps the `| \`key\` | default | meaning |` three-column shape.

The transaction producing a windowed document writes that pointer's signing.resign next run from it; a shortening (configure or rollback) marks every other signed pointer's row due now in the same transaction, so the cadence renews them at the next tick, off the request path; a lengthening changes nothing more. Hackage's request-path batch declined as option B. Generate reports a windowed document's expiry; AC22 window_change_test.go. AC23's Hackage takeover variant unconditional; optional cacheability citations folded.

## From the storage-and-gc.md closing sweep (2026-09-28, on Opus)
0. DATA-LOSS HOLE (fix before anything else): a checksum inside a document body is NOT a GC reference. Specs that keep an old blob "servable while a retained metadata revision names it" by naming it only inside a document will have that blob collected by the sweep while clients can still request it. Each must hold such blobs through a retained cached reference or the document's declared blob-digest list (the only keep-alive a document has). Affected: proxy-cache.md event-class table "new blob beside the old" (must say HOW the old blob is kept); arch.md and alpine.md (retained revisions and per-revision filename maps as CAS-backed metadata in a proxied repository's document); hackage.md (previous revision retained for its stale bound); rpm.md, alpine.md, arch.md, hackage.md, cpan.md (the new-blob-beside-old variant); debian.md (by-hash for previous generations relies on retained snapshots: for a pointer pinned to an aged snapshot those earlier generations are pruned, so the handler answers 404 when its by-hash map names a digest whose blob is gone, and apt falls back to the path, as captured).
1. data-model.md: the claim paragraph "storage-and-gc.md's sole write-transaction constructor is to carry (reported to it)" should cite storage-and-gc AC30, AC31 and was-Q12, and define the repository head as the default Pointer row.
2. management-api.md: "Retirement is core-held" and was-Q14 can cite storage-and-gc AC30 and was-Q12; unchanged-publish text can cite AC31; AC6's Test Plan row cites internal/storage/gc/arch_test.go but the file is internal/storage/arch_test.go.
3. question-triage.md: record storage-and-gc Q12 as adopted on Opus, awaiting Fable recheck.

3. alpine.md: AC20 "no upstream keys contributes no package" inverts; the alpine-keys recipe becomes advice for class signature; cite was-Q20, was-Q21 for the template (AC35).
4. cpan.md: AC26 shared directory: remote entries taken when no PAUSE key is configured, dropped under a configured key that fails (cure: keyserver import); Q12 record and Design; CHECKSUMS derivation cites signing-service was-Q21 (DeriveInputs).
5. debian.md: strict by construction (a remote with no keyring fails first fetch); cite was-Q20; "owed change" text for {suite} and by-hash derivation cites was-Q21, AC35; DeriveInputs is the envelope derivation.
6. cran.md, conda.md: "owed change" cites was-Q21, AC35; the read-driven first fetch for a remote-only tree or subdir now exists (state it, add to virtual_remote_test); "AC36 does not bind" is explicit in AC36.

8. rubygems.md: serve-policy asks met (signing-service AC30 body-md5 ETag and Repr-Digest) and Merge's previous-set input met (AC19); replace "consequence recorded in the report" wording; Q3 and Q7 records may cite.

14. repository-lifecycle.md: promotion and rollback rows: a repoint of a member's default pointer enqueues index.merge for every virtual listing it; virtual deletion ends the merged document's declared list.

## From the hackage.md Fable follow-up (2026-10-08, on Fable)
1. signing-service.md (round 8, DESIGN): the request-path re-sign batch the takeover runs (re-render every pointer through the door's document-only form and write the signing.resign next runs from the renewed expiry) must be reachable after a handler configure that shortens a validity window, because RegisterSchedules leaves an existing row untouched and a next run derived from the longer window fires after the shortened documents expired (hackage AC35; Debian's Valid-Until shortened by PATCH is a second citer). ## From the signing-service.md Fable follow-up round 8 (2026-10-08, on Fable)
1. hackage.md: service-list item 5, AC35 and its row, the Phase 3 gate and "Capabilities and lifecycle": after a `configure` that shortens a window, the idle pointers are renewed through the cadence at the next tick (their rows marked due in the configure's transaction), not re-rendered on the request path; the moved pointer is re-rendered with its row in the write. AC35's conformance case polls the idle pointer's version before reading. Replace "takes the takeover's shape on the request path". Phase 3 may claim AC35 (signing-service AC22 states the rule).
2. debian.md: a `valid_until` shortening by PATCH follows the same rule; AC10's row may cite `internal/signing/window_change_test.go`; its "window shorter than the cadence can keep alive" validation stands.
3. data-model.md: `PointerDocument` gains the document's expiry where windowed (signing-service AC36); `Generate`'s output reports it.
4. async-operations.md: "The scheduler" and AC14 say `Finish` rewrites next runs; now also the transaction producing a windowed document (its own pointer) and a shortening transaction (the others, to now). `RegisterSchedules` stays the only creator.
5. replication.md AC21 and management-api.md AC33: the Hackage takeover variant is unconditional (same as hackage follow-up item 2).
6. generic.md: its AC18 row names "signing-service AC38's head_test.go"; `head_test.go` is AC32's row and `cacheability_test.go` is AC38's.
